The show was originally scheduled to take place at the [Barclays Center](1658814.md) in [Brooklyn, New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn,_New_York_City), but was moved outdoors because of security concerns caused by the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md).[^cite_note-154] Instead, the 37th annual ceremony would "highlight the boroughs in an exciting show and return to Barclays Center in 2021".[^cite_note-155] For the first time in its history the VMAs were simulcast on over-the-air television on [The CW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_CW), which is jointly owned by MTV parent company [ViacomCBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ViacomCBS) and [AT\&T](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AT\&T) parent company [WarnerMedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WarnerMedia).[^cite_note-156]

[Keke Palmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keke_Palmer) hosted the show from a VR model of the infamous Manhattan staple, [Empire State Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_State_Building). [The Weeknd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Weeknd) won Video of the Year for "[Blinding Lights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blinding_Lights)", which he also performed as the show's opener. During his speech he showed support for the [Black Lives Matter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Lives_Matter) movement and demanded justice for [Breonna Taylor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shooting_of_Breonna_Taylor) and [Jacob Blake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shooting_of_Jacob_Blake).

[Lady Gaga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lady_Gaga) received the first-ever (and only thus far as of 2024) Tricon Award, introduced by [Bella Hadid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bella_Hadid). After giving her speech, Gaga performed a medley of her songs, including "[Chromatica II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/911_\(Lady_Gaga_song\)#%22Chromatica_II%22)"/"[911](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/911_\(Lady_Gaga_song\))", "[Stupid Love](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stupid_Love_\(Lady_Gaga_song\))" and "[Rain on Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rain_on_Me_\(Lady_Gaga_and_Ariana_Grande_song\))", the latter performed with [Ariana Grande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariana_Grande). Grande also won four awards, including Song of the Year and Best Collaboration.

The show was dedicated to actor [Chadwick Boseman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chadwick_Boseman), who died less than 48 hours before the ceremony due to colon cancer.



The redesigned moonperson trophy by Kehinde Wiley at the [2021 MTV Video Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_MTV_Video_Music_Awards)

**2021**: The [2021 MTV Video Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_MTV_Video_Music_Awards) were held September 12, 2021, at the [Barclays Center](1658814.md) in [Brooklyn, New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn,_New_York_City), for the first time in eight years.[^cite_note-billboard_vmas-157] The award show also served as a celebration of MTV's 40th anniversary. An opening monologue was delivered by [Madonna](142056.md).[^cite_note-158] Artist [Kehinde Wiley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kehinde_Wiley) redesigned the year's moonperson trophy, marking the third time that the statue had been transformed and the first from an artist of African-American descent.[^cite_note-159][^cite_note-moonman_redesign-160]

[Kim Petras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Petras) performed her single "[Future Starts Now](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Future_Starts_Now)", becoming the first openly transgender singer to perform at the VMAs.[^cite_note-161]

[Lil Nas X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lil_Nas_X) won three awards for "[Montero (Call Me By Your Name)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montero_\(Call_Me_By_Your_Name\))", including Video of the Year and Best Direction. He became the second artist and first LGBTQ+ artist to win both categories for a video they co-directed. He jokingly thanked the [gay agenda](1601956.md) in his acceptance speech.[^cite_note-162]

[Olivia Rodrigo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivia_Rodrigo) and [Justin Bieber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Bieber) also received awards in big categories, winning Song of the Year and Artist of the Year respectively. [BTS](39862325.md) won three awards, winning Group of the Year for the third consecutive time, in addition to Best K-Pop and Song of Summer for "[Butter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butter_\(song\))".[^cite_note-163]

**2022**: [Taylor Swift](5422144.md) announced the release of her tenth studio album *[Midnights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midnights)* during her Video of the Year award acceptance speech. Swift also became the first ever artist to win Video of the Year three times.

[Nicki Minaj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicki_Minaj) won the [Michael Jackson Video Vanguard award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson_Video_Vanguard_Award) that night and performed a 9-minute medley of her hits including "[Anaconda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anaconda_\(song\))," "[Super Bass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bass)", "[Monster"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monster_\(Kanye_West_song\)), "[Beez in the Trap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beez_in_the_Trap)", and her newest hit "[Super Freaky Girl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Freaky_Girl)" along with other songs. The award was presented by five members of her [fan club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fan_club) the Barbz. In her acceptance speech, Minaj shouted out multiple people who had inspired her and people who have given her big opportunities early in her career including [Madonna](142056.md), [Eminem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eminem), [Britney Spears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britney_Spears), [Lil Wayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lil_Wayne), [Drake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drake_\(musician\)), [Taylor Swift](5422144.md), [Beyoncé](83688.md), [Rihanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rihanna), and more who had help boost the popularity of her 2011 hit, "Super Bass". Minaj also said that she wished musicians [Whitney Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitney_Houston) and [Michael Jackson](14995351.md) were there to witness her.[^cite_note-164] She also paid respects to her father, Robert Maraj, who died in a tragic [hit-and-run](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hit_and_run) accident in 2021. She said she wished people took mental health more seriously. Minaj thanked the Barbz, told the audience about her son, nicknamed Papa Bear, and announced her first greatest hits album, *[Queen Radio Vol. 1.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Radio:_Volume_1)*

There were three co-hosts in the show, [LL Cool J](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LL_Cool_J), Minaj, and [Jack Harlow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Harlow).

Harlow opened the show with a performance of his single, "[First Class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Class_\(song\))". The performance took place on an airplane with Harlow acting as an [attendant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flight_attendant). Several celebrities were seated in the plane including Lil Nas X, [Saucy Santana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saucy_Santana), [Avril Lavigne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avril_Lavigne), and more. The plane "landed" at the award show and mocked a red carpet interview, with Harlow saying "Ladies and Gentlemen, [Fergie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fergie_\(singer\))!". Fergie had come out onstage to perform her 2007 hit "[Glamorous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glamorous_\(Fergie_song\))" which "First Class" samples, marking her first televised performance since her controversial performance of "[The Star-Spangled Banner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Star-Spangled_Banner)" at the [2018 NBA All Star Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_NBA_All_Star_Game). The performance ended with Fergie and Harlow hugging.

[Anitta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anitta_\(singer\)) becomes the first Brazilian singer to win a category at the MTV Video Music Awards.

**2023**: [NSYNC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NSYNC) reunited for the first time in 10 years at the ceremony, to present the award for [Best Pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Pop_Video), which was to [Taylor Swift](5422144.md)'s "[Anti-Hero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Hero_\(song\))". Swift acknowledged that she still has the 'N Sync dolls, referencing the "[It's Gonna Be Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/It's_Gonna_Be_Me)" music video, and they gave her friendship bracelets.

[Shakira](6479315.md) was awarded with the [Michael Jackson Video Vanguard Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson_Video_Vanguard_Award), performing a medley of her hits. She became the first South-American born artist to receive the award. Later, Shakira and [Karol G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karol_G) won the [Best Collaboration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Collaboration) award, for "[TQG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TQG)", making the first time a Spanish-language video and a Latin female collaboration won the award.

Taylor Swift won Video of the Year for a second consecutive year for "Anti-Hero", making her the artist with the most wins (four) in this category.

**2024**: The ceremony was originally scheduled to take place on September 10, 2024, but was pushed back by a day to avoid an overlap with the [presidential debate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_presidential_debates#September_10_presidential_debate) between [Donald Trump](4848272.md) and [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris).

Taylor Swift won seven awards, including Video of the Year and Artist of the Year, breaking the record for most awards won overall, with thirty wins since 2009. Her Video of the Year win for "[Fortnight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortnight_\(song\))" extended her record for the most wins and most consecutive wins in this category, with five wins and three years respectively.

**2025**: The [2025 Video Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_MTV_Video_Music_Awards) were held at the [UBS Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UBS_Arena) in [Elmont, New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elmont,_New_York), on September 7, 2025. It was broadcast for the first time on [CBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS) and available to stream on [Paramount+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount+) with a simulcast on [MTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV).[^cite_note-165][^cite_note-166] The show was hosted by rapper and actor [LL Cool J](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LL_Cool_J).[^cite_note-167]

[Lady Gaga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lady_Gaga) was the most nominated and awarded artist, winning four trophies out of twelve nominations. [Ariana Grande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariana_Grande)'s *[Brighter Days Ahead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brighter_Days_Ahead)* was crowned [Video of the Year](476903.md). [Mariah Carey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariah_Carey) was honored with the [Michael Jackson Video Vanguard Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson_Video_Vanguard_Award), presented by Grande. Two new special awards were given out; [Busta Rhymes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Busta_Rhymes) received the Rock the Bells Visionary, and [Ricky Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ricky_Martin) received the Latin Icon. The ceremony saw a 42% increase in viewership compared to the previous edition, its highest ratings in 6 years.[^cite_note-168]

**2026**: The VMA's return to the [Los Angeles Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Los_Angeles) since the [2017 MTV Video Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_MTV_Video_Music_Awards). The ceremony will be held at the [Peacock Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peacock_Theater) in [Downtown Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Los_Angeles) for the first time since the [2015 MTV Video Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_MTV_Video_Music_Awards) (back when it was named the Microsoft Theater). This will also be the first ceremony to be held in the fall.[^cite_note-169]

## Award categories

### Votable categories

Winners of the following categories are chosen by fan votes through a voting process.[^cite_note-203]

- [Video of the Year](476903.md)

- [Artist of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Artist_of_the_Year)

- [Song of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Awards_for_Song_of_the_Year)

- [Best New Artist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_New_Artist) (awarded as Best New Artist in a Video from 1984 to 2006, Artist to Watch from 2013 to 2015, and Push Best New Artist in 2020)

- [Best Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Group_Video) (awarded as Best Group Video from 1984 to 2007, and Group of the Year from 2021 to 2023)

- [Best Dance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Dance) (awarded as Best Dance Video from 1989 to 2006, Best Dancing in a Video in 2008, Best Dance Music Video in 2010, Best Electronic Dance Music Video in 2012, MTV Clubland Award in 2014, Best Electronic Video in 2016)

- [Best Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Rock_Video) (awarded as Best Heavy Metal Video in 1989, Best Heavy Metal/Hard Rock Video from 1990 to 1995, Best Hard Rock Video in 1996, and Best Rock Video from 1997 to 2016)

- [Best Alternative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Alternative_Video) (awarded as Best Alternative Video from 1991 to 1998)

- [Best Long Form Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Long_Form_Video) (awarded as Breakthrough Long Form Video in 2016)

- [Best R\&B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_R\&B_Video) (awarded as Best R\&B Video from 1993 to 2006)

- [Best Hip-Hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Hip-Hop_Video) (awarded as Best Hip-Hop Video from 1999 to 2016)

- [Best Pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Pop_Video) (awarded as Best Pop Video from 1999 to 2016)

- [Best Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Latino_Artist) (awarded as Best Latino Artist from 2010 to 2013)

- [Song of Summer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Song_of_Summer)

- [Best K-Pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_K-Pop_Video)

- [Best Album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Album_of_the_Year)

- [Best Country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CMT_Music_Award_for_Video_of_the_Year) (awarded as the [CMT Music Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CMT_Music_Awards) Video of the Year from 2002 to 2024)

### Professional categories

Winners of the following categories are chosen by members of the music industry.

- [Best Direction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Direction)

- [Best Choreography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Choreography)

- [Best Visual Effects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Visual_Effects) (awarded as Best Special Effects from 1984 to 2011)

- [Best Art Direction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Art_Direction)

- [Best Editing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Editing)

- [Best Cinematography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Cinematography)

### Special awards

The following awards have special significance and are not necessarily awarded annually.[^cite_note-204]

- **[Michael Jackson Video Vanguard Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson_Video_Vanguard_Award)** (awarded as the Video Vanguard Award from 1984 to 1990, 2006, awarded as the Lifetime Achievement an Award in 1994 and 2003)

- **Global Icon Award** was introduced in [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_MTV_Video_Music_Awards) and it is awarded to bands and musicians with "an unparalleled career and continued influence" and has achieved "global success in music and beyond."&#x20;

  - [Foo Fighters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foo_Fighters) (2021)

  - [Red Hot Chili Peppers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Hot_Chili_Peppers) (2022)

  - [P. Diddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P._Diddy) (2023)

- **MTV VMA Artist Director Honors** (2026)

### Past award categories

- Special Recognition Award (1984–1986)

- [Best Overall Performance in a Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Overall_Performance) (1984–1987)

- [Most Experimental Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Most_Experimental_Video) (1984–1987; replaced by Breakthrough Video)

- [Best Concept Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Concept_Video) (1984–1988)

- [Best Stage Performance in a Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Stage_Performance) (1984–1989)

- [Best Post-Modern Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Post-Modern_Video) (1989–1990; replaced by Best Alternative Video)

- [Best Artist Website](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Artist_Website) (1999)

- Best Video from a Film (1987–2003)

- [International Viewer's Choice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_%E2%80%93_International_Viewer's_Choice) (1989–2003; awards for individual regions were created and eliminated in different years)

- [Best Rap Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Rap_Video) (1989–2006)

- [MTV2 Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_%E2%80%93_MTV2_Award) (2001–2006)

- [Viewer's Choice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_%E2%80%93_Viewer's_Choice) (1984–2006)

- [Best Video Game Soundtrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Video_Game_Soundtrack) (2004–2006)

- [Best Video Game Score](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Video_Game_Score) (2006)

- [Ringtone of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Ringtone_of_the_Year) (2006)

- [Quadruple Threat of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Quadruple_Threat_of_the_Year) (2007)

- [Monster Single of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Monster_Single_of_the_Year) (2007)

- Best UK Video (2008)

- Best Video (That Should Have Won a Moonman) (2009)

- [Breakthrough Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_%E2%80%93_Breakthrough_Video) (1988–2005, 2009–2010)

- Most Share-Worthy Video (2012)

- [Best Latino Artist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Latino_Artist) (2010–2013)

- Best Lyric Video (2014)

- [Best Male Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Male_Video) (1984–2016; replaced by Artist of the Year)

- [Best Female Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Female_Video) (1984–2016; replaced by Artist of the Year)

- Push Artist of the Year (2018–2019; merged with [Best New Artist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_New_Artist) in 2020)

- Best Power Anthem (2019)

- MTV Fashion Trailbazer Award (2019)

- Best Music Video from Home (2020)

- Best Quarantine Performance (2020)

- Everyday Heroes: Frontline Medical Workers (2020)

- MTV Tricon Award (2020)

- VMAs Most Iconic Performance (2024)

- Best Trending Video (2024)

- Best Pop Artist (2025)

- Rock the Bells Visionary Award (2025)

- Latin Icon Award (2025)

- [Video for Good](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Video_for_Good) (2011–2025)

- Push Performance of the Year (2022–2025)

- [Best Afrobeats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award_for_Best_Afrobeats_Video) (2023-2025)

## Reception

The MTV Video Music Awards have been described as a prominent event in the mainstream [music industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_industry) since its inauguration. However, some publications such as *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* and *[HuffPost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HuffPost)* opined that the stature of the ceremony had declined by 2019.[^cite_note-WashPost-205][^cite_note-Status-206] *HuffPost* cited reasons such as a lack of interest (declining attendances and viewership: in 2019 ratings hit an all-time low for the third straight year),[^cite_note-207] lack of musical diversity, lack of celebrity, lack of credibility,[^cite_note-Status-206] and access to music online.[^cite_note-WashPost-205] *The Washington Post* states, "The moment the VMAs ceased to matter might have happened in 2014, when Drake didn't bother appearing to receive his award. Or maybe it was even earlier than that: The Associated Press compared the energy of the VMAs to the scripted reality-TV show *[Cribs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV_Cribs)* all the way back in 2006."[^cite_note-WashPost-205] MTV has also faced criticism for devoting most of its airtime outside the VMAs to reality shows and dramas, with music videos mainly airing in off-peak [graveyard slots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graveyard_slot) to suffice the minimum amount of music programming in the network's carriage agreements.[^cite_note-Status-206]

### Ratings

Ratings are not available prior to 1994 because MTV was not subscribing to [Nielsen Media Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nielsen_Media_Research)'s program ratings prior to 1994.[^cite_note-ratings-208] The field "Cumulative viewers" shows the audience of the live simulcasts of the ceremony across many ViacomCBS channels as a whole.
